People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I came in because… well, when I talk, it gets all tangled. Like I’ll start trying to explain the keys, and then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, you know—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. They tell me I’m jumping around too much, but in my head it feels connected. It’s been making things hard.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… even when I’m not saying it out loud, it can feel like it’s going in different directions. Like I’ll be thinking about the keys, because I need to explain the keys, and then somehow it turns into jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces… the horse one, the knight, I think.

And it makes sense for a second in my head, like there’s a string tying it together, but then when I try to hold onto it, it slips. People say they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I guess sometimes I can’t follow it clean either.

Yeah… sometimes. I hear it more than see things. Like a man’s voice, it’ll come from the vents or the mop closet at work, and I’ll think somebody’s in there, but there isn’t anybody. It says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Sometimes it’s clear, I guess… but a lot of the time when I’m trying to explain something, it tangles up. Like I start with the keys because that’s what I meant to say, then I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—the knight one, the horse—and I can see people looking at me like they don’t know where I went.

So… I don’t know if it’s ever completely clean. Maybe for little bits, but what brings me here is that my talking gets mixed up and people tell me they can’t follow me.

Yeah… yeah, they race. It’s like I’m trying to tell you one thing, and then there’s ten things pushing in at the same time. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head, not outside, but inside, and I can’t grab just one.

It feels like it’s talking to me, yeah… not just some pipe noise. I mean, I know vents rattle and the mop closet has that old door that sticks—I've cleaned that hallway a hundred times—but this is different.

Yeah… I mean, the main thing is when I’m trying to talk, it gets all mixed up. Like I’m trying to explain about the keys, and then somehow I’m on jail, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and I can see people just staring at me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if true is the right word… when it’s happening, it feels true. Like when the voice says my name, or says I ruined everything, it hits me like somebody is right there saying it.

Sometimes it sounds like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet—those vents make noises anyway, you know, in old buildings, dust and that metal ticking—but this is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it sounds real. So in that moment, yeah, I believe it, or I start believing it. Then later I try to tell myself maybe it was just the building, but it doesn’t feel like just noise when it happens.

Like I’m trying to explain about the keys, and then somehow I’m on jail, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and I can see people just staring at me.\n\nEven when I’m not meaning to explain it wrong, it comes out tangled. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and that’s… that’s really what brought me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if true is the right word… when it’s happening, it feels true. It’s a man’s voice, and it sounds real. So in that moment, yeah, I believe it, or I start believing it. Then later I try to tell myself maybe it was just the building, but it doesn’t feel like just noise when it happens.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think the voice you hear is trying to tell you something specific or send you a message?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because… well, when I talk, it gets all tangled. Like I’ll start trying to explain the keys, and then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, you know—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. They tell me I’m jumping around too much, but in my head it feels connected. It’s been making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties with your speech causing problems for you at work or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, work especially. I’ve missed shifts, and I lost some cleaning jobs because they said I wasn’t reliable, or they couldn’t understand what I was trying to tell them. Like if I was explaining the mop closet or the keys, it would turn into something else, and then people get nervous or mad.\n\nAnd with people… I don’t go to the mosque much now, or family gatherings, because they look at each other, like, “What is he saying?” My family says they can’t count on me sometimes. There were times it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help, which… I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your speech is clear and you can explain things without getting tangled, or is it a problem all the time?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can get a little clearer, yeah… like if it’s simple, just “the keys are on the hook,” I can say that. But then other times it gets mixed up fast. I’ll start with the keys and then I’m talking about jail, or the prayer rug, or those chess pieces—the horse one—and I know people can’t follow me. In my head it’s like there’s a path, but when it comes out, it’s all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to follow, even when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not saying it out loud, it can feel like it’s going in different directions. Like I’ll be thinking about the keys, because I need to explain the keys, and then somehow it turns into jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces… the horse one, the knight, I think.\n\nAnd it makes sense for a second in my head, like there’s a string tying it together, but then when I try to hold onto it, it slips. People say they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I guess sometimes I can’t follow it clean either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people do not?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I hear it more than see things. Like a man’s voice, it’ll come from the vents or the mop closet at work, and I’ll think somebody’s in there, but there isn’t anybody. It says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything.\n\nAnd it sounds real, like… not like a thought. Like somebody standing right beside me talking. The vents in those buildings, they carry sounds anyway, you know, old buildings, dust in them, the keys hanging by the closet… but this is different. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time, or is it something that happens occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not all the time… no. It’s more like it happens sometimes, but when it does, it’s strong. Like I’ll be near the vents, or that mop closet, and I hear this man’s voice say my name, or say I ruined everything.\n\nAnd I know buildings make noises, pipes and air and all that—I clean around them, I hear them all day—but this sounds like a person right there beside me. Then I start checking, like maybe somebody’s hiding, but… it’s not every minute. It just comes sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and speech are completely clear and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it’s clear, I guess… but a lot of the time when I’m trying to explain something, it tangles up. Like I start with the keys because that’s what I meant to say, then I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—the knight one, the horse—and I can see people looking at me like they don’t know where I went.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s ever completely clean. Maybe for little bits, but what brings me here is that my talking gets mixed up and people tell me they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or moving too fast when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, they race. It’s like I’m trying to tell you one thing, and then there’s ten things pushing in at the same time. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head, not outside, but inside, and I can’t grab just one.\n\nI’ll start with bills, then it jumps to jail, then the mosque, then the cleaning chemicals at work—bleach, ammonia, those warning labels—and I’m already three streets over from what I meant to say. It’s too fast. I know I’m supposed to slow it down, but it keeps moving before I can catch it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually talking to you or about you, or is it just a noise?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s talking to me, yeah… not just some pipe noise. I mean, I know vents rattle and the mop closet has that old door that sticks—I've cleaned that hallway a hundred times—but this is different.\n\nIt says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything, and it sounds like a man standing right beside me. Then I look around because I think, well, maybe someone’s there, maybe somebody slipped in… but I don’t see anybody. I’m not sure what counts as disorganized when I’m alone. I mean, nobody’s there to tell me I’m doing it wrong, right? Sometimes I’ll start cleaning one thing and then notice the sink, then the bucket, then I’m standing there holding a rag and I forgot why I had it… but that might just be how cleaning goes.

At home it’s harder to say. I’ll move stuff around and then not remember where I put it, but everybody does that. My mother used to keep coffee cans full of screws, all labeled wrong, and she always knew which one had the good nails in it—so maybe mess doesn’t always mean mess, you know?

So… I don’t know. Maybe sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you for sure unless somebody was watching.

It doesn’t feel like my thoughts, no… I mean, my thoughts sound like me, even when they’re bad. This is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it comes from places, like the vents or that mop closet, like somebody’s standing right there just out of sight.

In my head it’s like there’s a path, but when it comes out, it’s all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to follow, even when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not saying it out loud, it can feel like it’s going in different directions. People say they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I guess sometimes I can’t follow it clean either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people do not?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I hear it more than see things. Like a man’s voice, it’ll come from the vents or the mop closet at work, and I’ll think somebody’s in there, but there isn’t anybody. It says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything.\n\nAnd it sounds real, like… not like a thought. Like somebody standing right beside me talking. The vents in those buildings, they carry sounds anyway, you know, old buildings, dust in them, the keys hanging by the closet… but this is different. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time, or is it something that happens occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not all the time… no. It’s more like it happens sometimes, but when it does, it’s strong. Like I’ll be near the vents, or that mop closet, and I hear this man’s voice say my name, or say I ruined everything.\n\nAnd I know buildings make noises, pipes and air and all that—I clean around them, I hear them all day—but this sounds like a person right there beside me. Then I start checking, like maybe somebody’s hiding, but… it’s not every minute. It just comes sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and speech are completely clear and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it’s clear, I guess… but a lot of the time when I’m trying to explain something, it tangles up. Like I start with the keys because that’s what I meant to say, then I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—the knight one, the horse—and I can see people looking at me like they don’t know where I went.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s ever completely clean. Maybe for little bits, but what brings me here is that my talking gets mixed up and people tell me they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or moving too fast when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, they race. It’s like I’m trying to tell you one thing, and then there’s ten things pushing in at the same time. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head, not outside, but inside, and I can’t grab just one.\n\nI’ll start with bills, then it jumps to jail, then the mosque, then the cleaning chemicals at work—bleach, ammonia, those warning labels—and I’m already three streets over from what I meant to say. It’s too fast. I know I’m supposed to slow it down, but it keeps moving before I can catch it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually talking to you or about you, or is it just a noise?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s talking to me, yeah… not just some pipe noise. I mean, I know vents rattle and the mop closet has that old door that sticks—I've cleaned that hallway a hundred times—but this is different.\n\nIt says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything, and it sounds like a man standing right beside me. Then I look around because I think, well, maybe someone’s there, maybe somebody slipped in… but I don’t see anybody. Still, when it happens, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things in a disorganized or chaotic way when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main thing is when I’m trying to talk, it gets all mixed up. Like I’m trying to explain about the keys, and then somehow I’m on jail, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and I can see people just staring at me.\n\nEven when I’m not meaning to explain it wrong, it comes out tangled. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and that’s… that’s really what brought me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if true is the right word… when it’s happening, it feels true. Like when the voice says my name, or says I ruined everything, it hits me like somebody is right there saying it.\n\nSometimes it sounds like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet—those vents make noises anyway, you know, in old buildings, dust and that metal ticking—but this is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it sounds real. So in that moment, yeah, I believe it, or I start believing it. Then later I try to tell myself maybe it was just the building, but it doesn’t feel like just noise when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is trying to tell you something specific or send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I think it is. Not like instructions exactly, but it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and it feels like that means something, like it’s aimed right at me.\n\nIt’ll come from the vents sometimes, or over by the mop closet, which is funny because I know that closet makes noises—the door sticks, and there’s a little scrape on the floor where the bucket used to catch—but this isn’t that. It’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head like someone’s standing beside me. So yeah, it feels like it’s trying to tell me something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts and speech are completely normal and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes I start off okay, like if it’s just “the trash bags are in the closet” or something simple. But then when I try to really explain what happened, it gets tangled.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me here. I’ll be trying to talk about the keys, and then I’m talking about jail somehow, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. So completely normal? I don’t know. Maybe some parts of the day, but it doesn’t stay that way when I try to explain things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting or behaving in a disorganized way when you're alone or not trying to communicate with others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what counts as disorganized when I’m alone. I mean, nobody’s there to tell me I’m doing it wrong, right? Sometimes I’ll start cleaning one thing and then notice the sink, then the bucket, then I’m standing there holding a rag and I forgot why I had it… but that might just be how cleaning goes.\n\nAt home it’s harder to say. I’ll move stuff around and then not remember where I put it, but everybody does that. My mother used to keep coffee cans full of screws, all labeled wrong, and she always knew which one had the good nails in it—so maybe mess doesn’t always mean mess, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you for sure unless somebody was watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is a part of your own thoughts or something separate from your own mind?\n\nPatient: It doesn’t feel like my thoughts, no… I mean, my thoughts sound like me, even when they’re bad. This is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it comes from places, like the vents or that mop closet, like somebody’s standing right there just out of sight.\n\nSometimes it says my name, or says I ruined everything, and I’ll turn because it sounds real. Like when pipes knock in a building, you learn those sounds after years cleaning places, you know? This isn’t that. It feels separate from me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if it’s commanding like… “pick up the broom” or “go do this exact thing.” It’s more like it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and then I’ll stop what I’m doing because it sounds so close—like from the vent, or that mop closet, like someone’s right there.

And with vents, you know, in old buildings they carry sound funny. I used to think it was somebody upstairs or a radio left on, but it’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head. So it affects me that way, I guess. I listen for it. It makes me freeze up. But I don’t know about commands exactly.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F25.9, R47.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because… well, when I talk, it gets all tangled. Like I’ll start trying to explain the keys, and then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, you know—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. They tell me I’m jumping around too much, but in my head it feels connected. It’s been making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties with your speech causing problems for you at work or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, work especially. I’ve missed shifts, and I lost some cleaning jobs because they said I wasn’t reliable, or they couldn’t understand what I was trying to tell them. Like if I was explaining the mop closet or the keys, it would turn into something else, and then people get nervous or mad.\n\nAnd with people… I don’t go to the mosque much now, or family gatherings, because they look at each other, like, “What is he saying?” My family says they can’t count on me sometimes. There were times it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help, which… I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your speech is clear and you can explain things without getting tangled, or is it a problem all the time?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can get a little clearer, yeah… like if it’s simple, just “the keys are on the hook,” I can say that. But then other times it gets mixed up fast. I’ll start with the keys and then I’m talking about jail, or the prayer rug, or those chess pieces—the horse one—and I know people can’t follow me. In my head it’s like there’s a path, but when it comes out, it’s all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to follow, even when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not saying it out loud, it can feel like it’s going in different directions. Like I’ll be thinking about the keys, because I need to explain the keys, and then somehow it turns into jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces… the horse one, the knight, I think.\n\nAnd it makes sense for a second in my head, like there’s a string tying it together, but then when I try to hold onto it, it slips. People say they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I guess sometimes I can’t follow it clean either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people do not?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I hear it more than see things. Like a man’s voice, it’ll come from the vents or the mop closet at work, and I’ll think somebody’s in there, but there isn’t anybody. It says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything.\n\nAnd it sounds real, like… not like a thought. Like somebody standing right beside me talking. The vents in those buildings, they carry sounds anyway, you know, old buildings, dust in them, the keys hanging by the closet… but this is different. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time, or is it something that happens occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not all the time… no. It’s more like it happens sometimes, but when it does, it’s strong. Like I’ll be near the vents, or that mop closet, and I hear this man’s voice say my name, or say I ruined everything.\n\nAnd I know buildings make noises, pipes and air and all that—I clean around them, I hear them all day—but this sounds like a person right there beside me. Then I start checking, like maybe somebody’s hiding, but… it’s not every minute. It just comes sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and speech are completely clear and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it’s clear, I guess… but a lot of the time when I’m trying to explain something, it tangles up. Like I start with the keys because that’s what I meant to say, then I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—the knight one, the horse—and I can see people looking at me like they don’t know where I went.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s ever completely clean. Maybe for little bits, but what brings me here is that my talking gets mixed up and people tell me they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or moving too fast when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, they race. It’s like I’m trying to tell you one thing, and then there’s ten things pushing in at the same time. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head, not outside, but inside, and I can’t grab just one.\n\nI’ll start with bills, then it jumps to jail, then the mosque, then the cleaning chemicals at work—bleach, ammonia, those warning labels—and I’m already three streets over from what I meant to say. It’s too fast. I know I’m supposed to slow it down, but it keeps moving before I can catch it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually talking to you or about you, or is it just a noise?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s talking to me, yeah… not just some pipe noise. I mean, I know vents rattle and the mop closet has that old door that sticks—I've cleaned that hallway a hundred times—but this is different.\n\nIt says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything, and it sounds like a man standing right beside me. Then I look around because I think, well, maybe someone’s there, maybe somebody slipped in… but I don’t see anybody. Still, when it happens, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things in a disorganized or chaotic way when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main thing is when I’m trying to talk, it gets all mixed up. Like I’m trying to explain about the keys, and then somehow I’m on jail, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and I can see people just staring at me.\n\nEven when I’m not meaning to explain it wrong, it comes out tangled. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and that’s… that’s really what brought me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if true is the right word… when it’s happening, it feels true. Like when the voice says my name, or says I ruined everything, it hits me like somebody is right there saying it.\n\nSometimes it sounds like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet—those vents make noises anyway, you know, in old buildings, dust and that metal ticking—but this is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it sounds real. So in that moment, yeah, I believe it, or I start believing it. Then later I try to tell myself maybe it was just the building, but it doesn’t feel like just noise when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is trying to tell you something specific or send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I think it is. Not like instructions exactly, but it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and it feels like that means something, like it’s aimed right at me.\n\nIt’ll come from the vents sometimes, or over by the mop closet, which is funny because I know that closet makes noises—the door sticks, and there’s a little scrape on the floor where the bucket used to catch—but this isn’t that. It’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head like someone’s standing beside me. So yeah, it feels like it’s trying to tell me something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts and speech are completely normal and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes I start off okay, like if it’s just “the trash bags are in the closet” or something simple. But then when I try to really explain what happened, it gets tangled.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me here. I’ll be trying to talk about the keys, and then I’m talking about jail somehow, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. So completely normal? I don’t know. Maybe some parts of the day, but it doesn’t stay that way when I try to explain things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting or behaving in a disorganized way when you're alone or not trying to communicate with others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what counts as disorganized when I’m alone. I don’t know if there’s a trigger exactly. It feels like it just… knots up. I’ll be trying to explain one thing, like the keys, because the keys matter, you know, if you lose keys in a building everybody looks at you. They tell me, “I can’t follow what you’re saying,” and I get frustrated because in my head it all connects for a second. Then when I say it out loud, it comes out wrong, all crossed up. So I don’t know if it’s random or if something starts it. It just happens when I’m trying to explain.

Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “controlled” is the right word, but it feels like something outside me is pushing on them, you know? Like the building people had marked me somehow.

Even when my supervisor showed me the camera and said, “See, there’s nothing there,” I still couldn’t shake it. Because the hallway lights would blink, or people would give these little looks, and it felt like they were warning each other about me. Like they knew something and were passing it along without saying it.

And those lights, they always say it’s wiring, old buildings, ballasts going bad—I changed bulbs for years, I know how they sound when they buzz—but this felt different. Like it was aimed at me. So… yeah, influenced, maybe. Not like my hand being moved, but like my head couldn’t get away from what they were doing.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because… well, when I talk, it gets all tangled. Like I’ll start trying to explain the keys, and then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, you know—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. They tell me I’m jumping around too much, but in my head it feels connected. It’s been making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties with your speech causing problems for you at work or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, work especially. I’ve missed shifts, and I lost some cleaning jobs because they said I wasn’t reliable, or they couldn’t understand what I was trying to tell them. Like if I was explaining the mop closet or the keys, it would turn into something else, and then people get nervous or mad.\n\nAnd with people… I don’t go to the mosque much now, or family gatherings, because they look at each other, like, “What is he saying?” My family says they can’t count on me sometimes. There were times it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help, which… I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your speech is clear and you can explain things without getting tangled, or is it a problem all the time?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can get a little clearer, yeah… like if it’s simple, just “the keys are on the hook,” I can say that. But then other times it gets mixed up fast. I’ll start with the keys and then I’m talking about jail, or the prayer rug, or those chess pieces—the horse one—and I know people can’t follow me. In my head it’s like there’s a path, but when it comes out, it’s all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to follow, even when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not saying it out loud, it can feel like it’s going in different directions. People say they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I guess sometimes I can’t follow it clean either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people do not?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I hear it more than see things. Like a man’s voice, it’ll come from the vents or the mop closet at work, and I’ll think somebody’s in there, but there isn’t anybody. It says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything.\n\nAnd it sounds real, like… not like a thought. Like somebody standing right beside me talking. The vents in those buildings, they carry sounds anyway, you know, old buildings, dust in them, the keys hanging by the closet… but this is different. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time, or is it something that happens occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not all the time… no. It’s more like it happens sometimes, but when it does, it’s strong. Like I’ll be near the vents, or that mop closet, and I hear this man’s voice say my name, or say I ruined everything.\n\nAnd I know buildings make noises, pipes and air and all that—I clean around them, I hear them all day—but this sounds like a person right there beside me. Then I start checking, like maybe somebody’s hiding, but… it’s not every minute. It just comes sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and speech are completely clear and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it’s clear, I guess… but a lot of the time when I’m trying to explain something, it tangles up. Like I start with the keys because that’s what I meant to say, then I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—the knight one, the horse—and I can see people looking at me like they don’t know where I went.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s ever completely clean. Maybe for little bits, but what brings me here is that my talking gets mixed up and people tell me they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or moving too fast when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, they race. It’s like I’m trying to tell you one thing, and then there’s ten things pushing in at the same time. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head, not outside, but inside, and I can’t grab just one.\n\nI’ll start with bills, then it jumps to jail, then the mosque, then the cleaning chemicals at work—bleach, ammonia, those warning labels—and I’m already three streets over from what I meant to say. It’s too fast. I know I’m supposed to slow it down, but it keeps moving before I can catch it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually talking to you or about you, or is it just a noise?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s talking to me, yeah… not just some pipe noise. I mean, I know vents rattle and the mop closet has that old door that sticks—I've cleaned that hallway a hundred times—but this is different.\n\nIt says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything, and it sounds like a man standing right beside me. Then I look around because I think, well, maybe someone’s there, maybe somebody slipped in… but I don’t see anybody. Still, when it happens, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things in a disorganized or chaotic way when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main thing is when I’m trying to talk, it gets all mixed up. Like I’m trying to explain about the keys, and then somehow I’m on jail, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and I can see people just staring at me.\n\nEven when I’m not meaning to explain it wrong, it comes out tangled. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and that’s… that’s really what brought me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if true is the right word… when it’s happening, it feels true. Like when the voice says my name, or says I ruined everything, it hits me like somebody is right there saying it.\n\nSometimes it sounds like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet—those vents make noises anyway, you know, in old buildings, dust and that metal ticking—but this is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it sounds real. So in that moment, yeah, I believe it, or I start believing it. Then later I try to tell myself maybe it was just the building, but it doesn’t feel like just noise when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is trying to tell you something specific or send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I think it is. Not like instructions exactly, but it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and it feels like that means something, like it’s aimed right at me.\n\nIt’ll come from the vents sometimes, or over by the mop closet, which is funny because I know that closet makes noises—the door sticks, and there’s a little scrape on the floor where the bucket used to catch—but this isn’t that. It’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head like someone’s standing beside me. So yeah, it feels like it’s trying to tell me something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts and speech are completely normal and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes I start off okay, like if it’s just “the trash bags are in the closet” or something simple. But then when I try to really explain what happened, it gets tangled.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me here. I’ll be trying to talk about the keys, and then I’m talking about jail somehow, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. So completely normal? I don’t know. Maybe some parts of the day, but it doesn’t stay that way when I try to explain things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting or behaving in a disorganized way when you're alone or not trying to communicate with others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what counts as disorganized when I’m alone. I mean, nobody’s there to tell me I’m doing it wrong, right? Sometimes I’ll start cleaning one thing and then notice the sink, then the bucket, then I’m standing there holding a rag and I forgot why I had it… but that might just be how cleaning goes.\n\nAt home it’s harder to say. I’ll move stuff around and then not remember where I put it, but everybody does that. My mother used to keep coffee cans full of screws, all labeled wrong, and she always knew which one had the good nails in it—so maybe mess doesn’t always mean mess, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you for sure unless somebody was watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is a part of your own thoughts or something separate from your own mind?\n\nPatient: It doesn’t feel like my thoughts, no… I mean, my thoughts sound like me, even when they’re bad. Like when pipes knock in a building, you learn those sounds after years cleaning places, you know? This isn’t that. It feels separate from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is commanding you to do specific actions or influencing your behavior in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s commanding like… “pick up the broom” or “go do this exact thing.” It’s more like it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and then I’ll stop what I’m doing because it sounds so close—like from the vent, or that mop closet, like someone’s right there.\n\nAnd with vents, you know, in old buildings they carry sound funny. Then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, not the straight one—and people are staring at me.\n\nThey tell me, “I can’t follow what you’re saying,” and I get frustrated because in my head it all connects for a second. It just happens when I’m trying to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the thoughts in your head are being controlled or influenced by something outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “controlled” is the right word, but it feels like something outside me is pushing on them, you know? Like I’ll start trying to explain the keys, and then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, you know—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. They tell me I’m jumping around too much, but in my head it feels connected. It’s been making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties with your speech causing problems for you at work or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, work especially. I’ve missed shifts, and I lost some cleaning jobs because they said I wasn’t reliable, or they couldn’t understand what I was trying to tell them. Like if I was explaining the mop closet or the keys, it would turn into something else, and then people get nervous or mad.\n\nAnd with people… I don’t go to the mosque much now, or family gatherings, because they look at each other, like, “What is he saying?” My family says they can’t count on me sometimes. There were times it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help, which… I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your speech is clear and you can explain things without getting tangled, or is it a problem all the time?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can get a little clearer, yeah… like if it’s simple, just “the keys are on the hook,” I can say that. But then other times it gets mixed up fast. I’ll start with the keys and then I’m talking about jail, or the prayer rug, or those chess pieces—the horse one—and I know people can’t follow me. In my head it’s like there’s a path, but when it comes out, it’s all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to follow, even when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not saying it out loud, it can feel like it’s going in different directions. Like I’ll be thinking about the keys, because I need to explain the keys, and then somehow it turns into jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces… the horse one, the knight, I think.\n\nAnd it makes sense for a second in my head, like there’s a string tying it together, but then when I try to hold onto it, it slips. People say they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I guess sometimes I can’t follow it clean either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people do not?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I hear it more than see things. Like a man’s voice, it’ll come from the vents or the mop closet at work, and I’ll think somebody’s in there, but there isn’t anybody. It says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything.\n\nAnd it sounds real, like… not like a thought. Like somebody standing right beside me talking. The vents in those buildings, they carry sounds anyway, you know, old buildings, dust in them, the keys hanging by the closet… but this is different. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time, or is it something that happens occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not all the time… no. It’s more like it happens sometimes, but when it does, it’s strong. Like I’ll be near the vents, or that mop closet, and I hear this man’s voice say my name, or say I ruined everything.\n\nAnd I know buildings make noises, pipes and air and all that—I clean around them, I hear them all day—but this sounds like a person right there beside me. Then I start checking, like maybe somebody’s hiding, but… it’s not every minute. It just comes sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and speech are completely clear and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it’s clear, I guess… but a lot of the time when I’m trying to explain something, it tangles up. Like I start with the keys because that’s what I meant to say, then I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—the knight one, the horse—and I can see people looking at me like they don’t know where I went.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s ever completely clean. Maybe for little bits, but what brings me here is that my talking gets mixed up and people tell me they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or moving too fast when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, they race. It’s like I’m trying to tell you one thing, and then there’s ten things pushing in at the same time. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head, not outside, but inside, and I can’t grab just one.\n\nI’ll start with bills, then it jumps to jail, then the mosque, then the cleaning chemicals at work—bleach, ammonia, those warning labels—and I’m already three streets over from what I meant to say. It’s too fast. I know I’m supposed to slow it down, but it keeps moving before I can catch it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually talking to you or about you, or is it just a noise?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s talking to me, yeah… not just some pipe noise. I mean, I know vents rattle and the mop closet has that old door that sticks—I've cleaned that hallway a hundred times—but this is different.\n\nIt says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything, and it sounds like a man standing right beside me. Then I look around because I think, well, maybe someone’s there, maybe somebody slipped in… but I don’t see anybody. Still, when it happens, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things in a disorganized or chaotic way when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main thing is when I’m trying to talk, it gets all mixed up. Like I’m trying to explain about the keys, and then somehow I’m on jail, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and I can see people just staring at me.\n\nEven when I’m not meaning to explain it wrong, it comes out tangled. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and that’s… that’s really what brought me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if true is the right word… when it’s happening, it feels true. Like when the voice says my name, or says I ruined everything, it hits me like somebody is right there saying it.\n\nSometimes it sounds like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet—those vents make noises anyway, you know, in old buildings, dust and that metal ticking—but this is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it sounds real. So in that moment, yeah, I believe it, or I start believing it. Then later I try to tell myself maybe it was just the building, but it doesn’t feel like just noise when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is trying to tell you something specific or send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I think it is. Not like instructions exactly, but it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and it feels like that means something, like it’s aimed right at me.\n\nIt’ll come from the vents sometimes, or over by the mop closet, which is funny because I know that closet makes noises—the door sticks, and there’s a little scrape on the floor where the bucket used to catch—but this isn’t that. It’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head like someone’s standing beside me. So yeah, it feels like it’s trying to tell me something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts and speech are completely normal and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes I start off okay, like if it’s just “the trash bags are in the closet” or something simple. But then when I try to really explain what happened, it gets tangled.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me here. I’ll be trying to talk about the keys, and then I’m talking about jail somehow, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. So completely normal? I don’t know. Maybe some parts of the day, but it doesn’t stay that way when I try to explain things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting or behaving in a disorganized way when you're alone or not trying to communicate with others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what counts as disorganized when I’m alone. I mean, nobody’s there to tell me I’m doing it wrong, right? Sometimes I’ll start cleaning one thing and then notice the sink, then the bucket, then I’m standing there holding a rag and I forgot why I had it… but that might just be how cleaning goes.\n\nAt home it’s harder to say. I’ll move stuff around and then not remember where I put it, but everybody does that. My mother used to keep coffee cans full of screws, all labeled wrong, and she always knew which one had the good nails in it—so maybe mess doesn’t always mean mess, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you for sure unless somebody was watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is a part of your own thoughts or something separate from your own mind?\n\nPatient: It doesn’t feel like my thoughts, no… I mean, my thoughts sound like me, even when they’re bad. This is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it comes from places, like the vents or that mop closet, like somebody’s standing right there just out of sight.\n\nSometimes it says my name, or says I ruined everything, and I’ll turn because it sounds real. Like when pipes knock in a building, you learn those sounds after years cleaning places, you know? This isn’t that. It feels separate from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is commanding you to do specific actions or influencing your behavior in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s commanding like… “pick up the broom” or “go do this exact thing.” It’s more like it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and then I’ll stop what I’m doing because it sounds so close—like from the vent, or that mop closet, like someone’s right there.\n\nAnd with vents, you know, in old buildings they carry sound funny. I used to think it was somebody upstairs or a radio left on, but it’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head. So it affects me that way, I guess. I listen for it. It makes me freeze up. But I don’t know about commands exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any idea what triggers these episodes of tangled speech and thoughts, or do they seem to happen randomly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s a trigger exactly. It feels like it just… knots up. I’ll be trying to explain one thing, like the keys, because the keys matter, you know, if you lose keys in a building everybody looks at you. Then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, not the straight one—and people are staring at me.\n\nThey tell me, “I can’t follow what you’re saying,” and I get frustrated because in my head it all connects for a second. Then when I say it out loud, it comes out wrong, all crossed up. So I don’t know if it’s random or if something starts it. It just happens when I’m trying to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the thoughts in your head are being controlled or influenced by something outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “controlled” is the right word, but it feels like something outside me is pushing on them, you know? Like the building people had marked me somehow.\n\nEven when my supervisor showed me the camera and said, “See, there’s nothing there,” I still couldn’t shake it. Because the hallway lights would blink, or people would give these little looks, and it felt like they were warning each other about me. Like they knew something and were passing it along without saying it.\n\nAnd those lights, they always say it’s wiring, old buildings, ballasts going bad—I changed bulbs for years, I know how they sound when they buzz—but this felt different. Like it was aimed at me. So… yeah, influenced, maybe. Not like my hand being moved, but like my head couldn’t get away from what they were doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear and your tangled thoughts are related to each other?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I don’t know if the voice causes it, or if it’s just all happening at once. What brings me here is my talking gets all mixed up. I try to explain about the keys, and then I’m talking about the jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like I said, the horse one—and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nSo maybe it’s related, because when I’m trying to explain it, it gets tangled. But I can’t say for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… sometimes it is. Not every time, but sometimes it’ll be like a man’s voice, like it’s coming through the vents, or from the mop closet — and I know how vents sound, I cleaned around them for years, they rattle different when the heat kicks on — but this sounds like a person.

It says my name sometimes. Or it says I ruined everything. Like right next to me, not like a thought. Like somebody is standing there by my shoulder, and then I look and there’s nobody there. That’s the part that gets me, because the mop closet is small, you know, there’s buckets and the yellow signs and all that, not really room for somebody to hide, but it still sounds real.
=====================================

---------- turn 20 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F25.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because… well, when I talk, it gets all tangled. Like I’ll start trying to explain the keys, and then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, you know—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. They tell me I’m jumping around too much, but in my head it feels connected. It’s been making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Are these difficulties with your speech causing problems for you at work or in your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I mean, work especially. I’ve missed shifts, and I lost some cleaning jobs because they said I wasn’t reliable, or they couldn’t understand what I was trying to tell them. Like if I was explaining the mop closet or the keys, it would turn into something else, and then people get nervous or mad.\n\nAnd with people… I don’t go to the mosque much now, or family gatherings, because they look at each other, like, “What is he saying?” My family says they can’t count on me sometimes. There were times it got bad enough that other people had to call for emergency help, which… I don’t like saying that, but yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your speech is clear and you can explain things without getting tangled, or is it a problem all the time?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I can get a little clearer, yeah… like if it’s simple, just “the keys are on the hook,” I can say that. But then other times it gets mixed up fast. I’ll start with the keys and then I’m talking about jail, or the prayer rug, or those chess pieces—the horse one—and I know people can’t follow me. In my head it’s like there’s a path, but when it comes out, it’s all tangled.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to follow, even when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even when I’m not saying it out loud, it can feel like it’s going in different directions. Like I’ll be thinking about the keys, because I need to explain the keys, and then somehow it turns into jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces… the horse one, the knight, I think.\n\nAnd it makes sense for a second in my head, like there’s a string tying it together, but then when I try to hold onto it, it slips. People say they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I guess sometimes I can’t follow it clean either.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people do not?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I hear it more than see things. Like a man’s voice, it’ll come from the vents or the mop closet at work, and I’ll think somebody’s in there, but there isn’t anybody. It says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything.\n\nAnd it sounds real, like… not like a thought. Like somebody standing right beside me talking. The vents in those buildings, they carry sounds anyway, you know, old buildings, dust in them, the keys hanging by the closet… but this is different. It feels like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the man's voice all the time, or is it something that happens occasionally?\n\nPatient: Not all the time… no. It’s more like it happens sometimes, but when it does, it’s strong. Like I’ll be near the vents, or that mop closet, and I hear this man’s voice say my name, or say I ruined everything.\n\nAnd I know buildings make noises, pipes and air and all that—I clean around them, I hear them all day—but this sounds like a person right there beside me. Then I start checking, like maybe somebody’s hiding, but… it’s not every minute. It just comes sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have times when your thoughts and speech are completely clear and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: Sometimes it’s clear, I guess… but a lot of the time when I’m trying to explain something, it tangles up. Like I start with the keys because that’s what I meant to say, then I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—the knight one, the horse—and I can see people looking at me like they don’t know where I went.\n\nSo… I don’t know if it’s ever completely clean. Maybe for little bits, but what brings me here is that my talking gets mixed up and people tell me they can’t follow me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are racing or moving too fast when you're trying to explain something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, they race. It’s like I’m trying to tell you one thing, and then there’s ten things pushing in at the same time. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head, not outside, but inside, and I can’t grab just one.\n\nI’ll start with bills, then it jumps to jail, then the mosque, then the cleaning chemicals at work—bleach, ammonia, those warning labels—and I’m already three streets over from what I meant to say. It’s too fast. I know I’m supposed to slow it down, but it keeps moving before I can catch it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is actually talking to you or about you, or is it just a noise?\n\nPatient: It feels like it’s talking to me, yeah… not just some pipe noise. I mean, I know vents rattle and the mop closet has that old door that sticks—I've cleaned that hallway a hundred times—but this is different.\n\nIt says my name sometimes, or says I ruined everything, and it sounds like a man standing right beside me. Then I look around because I think, well, maybe someone’s there, maybe somebody slipped in… but I don’t see anybody. Still, when it happens, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing things in a disorganized or chaotic way when you're not trying to explain something to someone else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, the main thing is when I’m trying to talk, it gets all mixed up. Like I’m trying to explain about the keys, and then somehow I’m on jail, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and I can see people just staring at me.\n\nEven when I’m not meaning to explain it wrong, it comes out tangled. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and that’s… that’s really what brought me here.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if true is the right word… when it’s happening, it feels true. Like when the voice says my name, or says I ruined everything, it hits me like somebody is right there saying it.\n\nSometimes it sounds like it’s coming from the vents, or the mop closet—those vents make noises anyway, you know, in old buildings, dust and that metal ticking—but this is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it sounds real. So in that moment, yeah, I believe it, or I start believing it. Then later I try to tell myself maybe it was just the building, but it doesn’t feel like just noise when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is trying to tell you something specific or send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes I think it is. Not like instructions exactly, but it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and it feels like that means something, like it’s aimed right at me.\n\nIt’ll come from the vents sometimes, or over by the mop closet, which is funny because I know that closet makes noises—the door sticks, and there’s a little scrape on the floor where the bucket used to catch—but this isn’t that. It’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head like someone’s standing beside me. So yeah, it feels like it’s trying to tell me something about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts and speech are completely normal and organized, without any tangling or disorganization?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe sometimes I start off okay, like if it’s just “the trash bags are in the closet” or something simple. But then when I try to really explain what happened, it gets tangled.\n\nThat’s kind of what brought me here. I’ll be trying to talk about the keys, and then I’m talking about jail somehow, and then the prayer rug, and then those chess pieces—the horse one—and people look at me like they can’t follow it. So completely normal? I don’t know. Maybe some parts of the day, but it doesn’t stay that way when I try to explain things.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself acting or behaving in a disorganized way when you're alone or not trying to communicate with others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what counts as disorganized when I’m alone. I mean, nobody’s there to tell me I’m doing it wrong, right? Sometimes I’ll start cleaning one thing and then notice the sink, then the bucket, then I’m standing there holding a rag and I forgot why I had it… but that might just be how cleaning goes.\n\nAt home it’s harder to say. I’ll move stuff around and then not remember where I put it, but everybody does that. My mother used to keep coffee cans full of screws, all labeled wrong, and she always knew which one had the good nails in it—so maybe mess doesn’t always mean mess, you know?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe sometimes, but I couldn’t tell you for sure unless somebody was watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is a part of your own thoughts or something separate from your own mind?\n\nPatient: It doesn’t feel like my thoughts, no… I mean, my thoughts sound like me, even when they’re bad. This is different. It’s a man’s voice, and it comes from places, like the vents or that mop closet, like somebody’s standing right there just out of sight.\n\nSometimes it says my name, or says I ruined everything, and I’ll turn because it sounds real. Like when pipes knock in a building, you learn those sounds after years cleaning places, you know? This isn’t that. It feels separate from me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is commanding you to do specific actions or influencing your behavior in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s commanding like… “pick up the broom” or “go do this exact thing.” It’s more like it says my name, or it says I ruined everything, and then I’ll stop what I’m doing because it sounds so close—like from the vent, or that mop closet, like someone’s right there.\n\nAnd with vents, you know, in old buildings they carry sound funny. I used to think it was somebody upstairs or a radio left on, but it’s a man’s voice, clear enough that I turn my head. So it affects me that way, I guess. I listen for it. It makes me freeze up. But I don’t know about commands exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any idea what triggers these episodes of tangled speech and thoughts, or do they seem to happen randomly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s a trigger exactly. It feels like it just… knots up. I’ll be trying to explain one thing, like the keys, because the keys matter, you know, if you lose keys in a building everybody looks at you. Then somehow I’m talking about jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like the little horse one, not the straight one—and people are staring at me.\n\nThey tell me, “I can’t follow what you’re saying,” and I get frustrated because in my head it all connects for a second. Then when I say it out loud, it comes out wrong, all crossed up. So I don’t know if it’s random or if something starts it. It just happens when I’m trying to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the thoughts in your head are being controlled or influenced by something outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “controlled” is the right word, but it feels like something outside me is pushing on them, you know? Like the building people had marked me somehow.\n\nEven when my supervisor showed me the camera and said, “See, there’s nothing there,” I still couldn’t shake it. Because the hallway lights would blink, or people would give these little looks, and it felt like they were warning each other about me. Like they knew something and were passing it along without saying it.\n\nAnd those lights, they always say it’s wiring, old buildings, ballasts going bad—I changed bulbs for years, I know how they sound when they buzz—but this felt different. Like it was aimed at me. So… yeah, influenced, maybe. Not like my hand being moved, but like my head couldn’t get away from what they were doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear and your tangled thoughts are related to each other?\n\nPatient: Maybe. I don’t know if the voice causes it, or if it’s just all happening at once. What brings me here is my talking gets all mixed up. I try to explain about the keys, and then I’m talking about the jail, and then the prayer rug, and then chess pieces—like I said, the horse one—and people tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying.\n\nSo maybe it’s related, because when I’m trying to explain it, it gets tangled. But I can’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is ever critical or threatening towards you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it is. Not every time, but sometimes it’ll be like a man’s voice, like it’s coming through the vents, or from the mop closet — and I know how vents sound, I cleaned around them for years, they rattle different when the heat kicks on — but this sounds like a person.\n\nIt says my name sometimes. Or it says I ruined everything. Like right next to me, not like a thought. Like somebody is standing there by my shoulder, and then I look and there’s nobody there. That’s the part that gets me, because the mop closet is small, you know, there’s buckets and the yellow signs and all that, not really room for somebody to hide, but it still sounds real.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Not every time, but sometimes it’ll be like a man’s voice, like it’s coming through the vents, or from the mop closet — and I know how vents sound, I cleaned around them for years, they rattle different when the heat kicks on — but this sounds like a person.\n\nIt says my name sometimes. Or it says I ruined everything. Like right next to me, not like a thought. Like somebody is standing there by my shoulder, and then I look and there’s nobody there. That’s the part that gets me, because the mop closet is small, you know, there’s buckets and the yellow signs and all that, not really room for somebody to hide, but it still sounds real."
